| D-156 | `requireDatabaseUrl()` (`lib/data/connection.ts`), called eagerly at MODULE IMPORT time via `hmrSingleton`'s immediate `create()` invocation, threw if `APP_DATABASE_URL`/`DATABASE_URL` were unset -- and `next build`'s "Collecting page data" phase imports every route module, so the throw happened DURING THE BUILD, not merely at first real use | BUG | 2026-08-08 | **CORRECTION to this row's own original framing, made by the coordinator and confirmed live: this was NOT pre-existing and NOT independent of Task 6/this project's own work -- it is a reach consequence of D-149's fix, three steps removed, and the original wording ("almost certainly fails today, independent of anything Task 6 touched") would have sent whoever picked this up looking in the wrong place.** The real chain: (1) Task 2 found `.env` being copied into the shipped Docker image; (2) the coordinator ordered a `.dockerignore` excluding `.env` -- correct, and it closed a real secret leak (**see D-149**, which this row is the direct downstream consequence of); (3) that ALSO removed `.env` from the Docker **builder** stage, not only the runner -- before it, `docker/Dockerfile:18`'s `COPY . .` put `DATABASE_URL` into the build environment, and the leak was, accidentally, what kept `next build` working; (4) `requireDatabaseUrl` was (see fix below) evaluated at IMPORT time, and `next build` imports every route handler to collect page data. Every individual decision in that chain -- fixing the leak, writing `requireDatabaseUrl`'s eager-fail-loud check in the first place (D-086) -- was correct on its own; the COMPOSITION was not assessed, and this row exists because nobody had, until the coordinator actually ran `next build` with `.env` moved aside and reproduced: `Error: DATABASE_URL is not set. ... Failed to collect page data for /api/users/me` | **FIXED, this round.** Generalised the SAME lazy-initialisation pattern already used for `lib/auth.ts`'s `requireAuthSecret()` (D-114) to `hmrSingleton` itself (`lib/data/connection.ts`) -- a `Proxy` deferring `create()` (and therefore `requireDatabaseUrl`'s check) to the FIRST actual property access, not import time, since `hmrSingleton` has no framework-provided lazy hook of its own to delegate to the way `NextAuth()` did. Applied to ALL FOUR current callers (`lib/data/tenant.ts#appClient`, `lib/data/identity.ts#base`+`identityDb`, `lib/data/preauth.ts#ownerClient`) via the ONE shared helper, with ZERO call-site changes anywhere (verified by enumerating all ~15 real usage sites across the codebase before fixing, not assumed transparent). A full sweep for siblings (every `hmrSingleton`/`requireDatabaseUrl`/`requireAuthSecret` caller, every raw `new PrismaClient`/`new Pool`/`new PrismaPg` construction site, every module-top-level bare `throw`, `lib/email/send.ts`'s `RESEND_API_KEY` check, `members/route.ts`'s `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS` check, the Prisma generator config, a search for any other `middleware.ts`) found NOTHING ELSE of this shape -- both `requireAuthSecret()` call sites were already correctly lazy from the prior fix round, and the two OTHER required-env-var checks (`RESEND_API_KEY`, `EMAIL_FROM_ADDRESS`) were already function-scoped, not module-scope. **Verified both directions with real, isolated `next build` runs** (`.env`-excluded context matching `.dockerignore`, hardlinked `node_modules`, `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` supplied to isolate on this specific variable): succeeds with `DATABASE_URL`/`APP_DATABASE_URL` absent; reverting to the eager form in the IDENTICAL environment fails with the identical error the coordinator found, isolating the fix as the actual cause. **Verified the guard still fires at RUNTIME, not just that the build no longer breaks** (the coordinator's own explicit concern -- "a lazy guard that never fires is worse than an eager one that fires too early"): a fresh Node process, no `DATABASE_URL`, imports `lib/data/tenant.ts` without throwing, then throws the exact `requireDatabaseUrl` message on the FIRST real `appClient.$transaction(...)` call, and throws again on a second attempt (does not cache a broken state as success). Also caught, by the same enumeration/mutation traps the fix needed for `identityDb`'s frozen surface: without an `ownKeys`/`getOwnPropertyDescriptor` trap, `Object.keys(identityDb)` silently answered against the Proxy's own empty target instead of the real, lazily-constructed instance -- `__tests__/integration/data-boundary.test.ts`'s "exposes exactly the constructed surface" test (a five-attempt-documented security pin) caught this on the first full-suite run after the initial (get/has-only) version of the fix, and the additional traps needed for it are now covered by dedicated tests in `__tests__/unit/connection.test.ts`, not just inferred safe | Phase 1c | Closed-done. See D-149 for the leak this is downstream of |
| D-157 | `hmrSingleton` (`lib/data/connection.ts`) stopped being a singleton in production. Fix round 2's lazy `Proxy` made every trap call `getOrCreate()`, whose only memo was the `globalThis` write guarded by `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` -- so in the deployed container (`docker/Dockerfile:26` sets `ENV NODE_ENV=production`) `create()` re-ran on EVERY property access, building a fresh `PrismaClient` and `pg.Pool` that nothing ever closes | BUG | 2026-08-09 | Found by the coordinator's scoped re-review of fix round 2, and reproduced independently in a real node process before anything was changed: `NODE_ENV=production create() invoked 3 time(s)` for 3 property touches, against `1 time(s)` under `test`, `development` and unset. **The condition on that line was never edited -- its MEANING inverted.** Pre-Proxy, `create()` ran once eagerly at import and the production singleton WAS the caller's own `export const appClient = hmrSingleton(...)` binding; the `globalThis` write only ever existed to survive dev hot-reload re-evaluating the module. Round 2 moved construction behind the Proxy, so the const stopped being the memo, and nothing replaced it in the one environment that ships -- the same shape AGENTS.md §3 names, a control whose enforcement point moved while its trigger stayed behind. Consequence on the tenant spine: `lib/data/tenant.ts:209`'s `withOrg()`, which that file's own header calls the ONLY path to tenant data, does `appClient.$transaction(...)` -- one property access per request -- so every tenant-scoped request built a new pool (`appClient` max 10, `identityDb`'s base and `ownerClient` max 5 each) with no `$disconnect()` or `.end()` anywhere, exhausting Postgres `max_connections` and leaking sockets under real traffic. SECOND-ORDER, and worse than the leak: `identityVerified` (`lib/data/tenant.ts:144`) is a module-scoped promise memo, so `assertRestrictedIdentity` -- the one check that verifies the RLS role assumption against reality rather than against configuration -- ran against the FIRST client only, and every later per-request client went entirely unverified. Why no test caught it: `__tests__/unit/connection.test.ts` stubbed `NODE_ENV` to `test` in a single file-wide `beforeEach`, so its own test titled exactly-once-across-multiple-property-accesses was vacuous for the only environment that matters | **FIXED, this round.** Added a module-scoped `Map` memo inside `lib/data/connection.ts` as tier 1 -- the SAME storage tier the pre-Proxy code used, since the caller's `export const` binding is equally module-scoped -- written only AFTER `create()` returns, so a throwing `create()` still re-attempts and `requireDatabaseUrl`'s D-086 check can never be skipped by a cached half-construction. The `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` condition on the `globalThis` write is UNCHANGED, deliberately: that write exists only to bridge dev hot-reload re-evaluating the module, and writing it in production would publish an ambient import-free handle to a live database client on the tenant-isolation spine, reachable without going through `withOrg`. TDD, red before green, and mechanism preferred over patch -- the test file's environment is now a GENERATED dimension (`describe.each` over production, development, test and undefined) instead of a hardcoded constant, so an environment-sensitive branch can no longer leave the deployed branch untested by construction. Non-vacuity proven three ways by deliberate reversal: removing the new memo tier reds exactly 4 tests and all 4 are in the production block; deleting the `NODE_ENV` condition reds exactly the 2 globalThis-hygiene tests; removing the `globalThis` write entirely reds exactly the 2 hot-reload tests -- so the three concerns are independently pinned, not one assertion wearing three hats. `identityDb`'s surface confirmed byte-for-byte identical pre- and post-fix by a real ES-module node process under `NODE_ENV=production`, and `npx next build` with `.env` moved aside still exits 0. Class closed as well as instance: a generated sweep finds this is the ONLY `process.env.NODE_ENV` branch in production code repo-wide | Phase 1c | Closed-done. See D-156 for the lazy Proxy this regressed, and D-158 for the one property deliberately left unchanged |
| D-158 | `hmrSingleton`'s production memo is module-scoped, so it deduplicates within one module instance but not across two. If a bundler emits `lib/data/connection.ts` into two chunks that both load in one server process, each copy constructs its own `PrismaClient` and `pg.Pool` | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Not a regression and not introduced by D-157's fix: the pre-Proxy code had the identical property, because its production memo was the caller's `export const appClient = hmrSingleton(...)` binding, which is equally per-module-instance. The only way to deduplicate ACROSS module instances is to write to `globalThis` in production, which would supersede that write's documented hygiene intent and publish an ambient import-free handle to a live database client on the tenant-isolation spine -- a worse trade on this codebase than a small bounded number of pools. Scoped with evidence rather than assumed either way: `grep -rl "Refusing to construct a connection pool" .next/standalone` over a real `next build` output returns 3 emitted server chunks (`.next/standalone/.next/server/chunks/_1mzpq6c._.js` plus two under `chunks/ssr/`), so the duplication is REAL in the artifact. What is NOT measured is whether more than one of them is ever instantiated in a single running process, which needs a running container to answer and is therefore not something this task could settle. Bounded either way -- at most one client per loaded chunk group, a small constant, NOT per-request as D-157 was -- and pinned as explicit tested behaviour rather than left an unstated assumption | First deployment of the container under real traffic: query `pg_stat_activity` grouped by `usename` and `client_addr` and compare the backend count from one container against the configured pool maxima (`appClient` 10 plus `identityDb` base 5 plus `ownerClient` 5). More than one pool's worth per client key confirms multi-instantiation and this row becomes a real defect. Also re-open on any change to `next.config.ts`'s bundling or `output` mode, or a Next.js major upgrade, since either can change how chunks are split | Phase 4 | Open |
| D-159 | `hmrSingleton`'s re-attempt-on-failure guarantee (`lib/data/connection.ts`) holds for a SYNCHRONOUS throw only. A `create()` that returned a rejected Promise would be memoised as a success, so `requireDatabaseUrl` would never re-run and the rejection would surface as an unhandled rejection uncorrelated with the access that caused it | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by `pr-review-toolkit:silent-failure-hunter` on fix round 4's change and verified by execution, not by reading: a `create()` returning a rejected Promise is called once across two accesses, where a synchronously-throwing one is called twice. NOT reachable today and that is why it is a row rather than a fix -- all four current `create()` functions are synchronous `new PrismaClient(...)` constructions, and `hmrSingleton`'s signature is `create: () => T` with `T extends object`, so nothing can currently produce this shape. Building async-rejection handling for a caller that does not exist would be speculative machinery on the most safety-critical function in the data layer, which is a worse trade than a row with a sharp trigger. What WAS done instead: the doc comment's previously unqualified claim -- that construction re-attempts "until construction actually succeeds" -- is now explicitly scoped to synchronous throws and points here, so the next author meets the limitation at the moment they would otherwise rely on the false version | Before making ANY `create()` passed to `hmrSingleton` asynchronous -- the concrete near case is fetching a connection string from a secrets manager rather than an environment variable, which the D-149 secret-handling work makes plausible. Also fires if `hmrSingleton`'s signature is ever widened past `() => T` where `T extends object`. At that moment the memo must await and must not cache a rejection, and the fail-closed tests in `__tests__/unit/connection.test.ts` must gain async counterparts | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-160 | `hmrSingleton` keys are bare unnamespaced strings in a process-wide registry that is now active in production, so two call sites sharing a key silently share one client -- and the four keys do not all carry the same database authority | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by `pr-review-toolkit:silent-failure-hunter` as the REACH of D-157's fix rather than a defect in it, and it is genuinely new: before D-157 production had no cross-call-site memo at all, so each site got its own client from its own `create()` and a shared key was merely wasteful. Now the second caller silently receives the FIRST caller's client, its `create()` never invoked, no error anywhere. The authority difference is what makes it matter: `appClient` is built on `APP_DATABASE_URL` (`makrai_app`, NOBYPASSRLS, the identity the entire tenant boundary depends on) while `identityBaseClient` and `preauthClient` are built on `DATABASE_URL`, the schema OWNER, which is BYPASSRLS -- so a collision could substitute a BYPASSRLS client for the RLS-enforced one with `requireDatabaseUrl('APP_DATABASE_URL')` never running. Calibrated rather than inflated: the dangerous direction is backstopped by `assertRestrictedIdentity` (`lib/data/tenant.ts`), which throws on `rolsuper`/`rolbypassrls` inside the transaction, and the reverse direction fails closed to zero rows. A latent trap, not a live hole | **MITIGATED this round, not merely noted.** A generated uniqueness check now lives in `__tests__/integration/trigger-enumeration.test.ts` -- it greps the `hmrSingleton` call sites from disk, asserts the keys are all distinct, and pins the exact expected set so ADDING a client is a conscious decision that updates the test. Proven non-vacuous by temporarily renaming `preauth.ts`'s key to `appClient`, which reds it on the distinctness assertion. Row stays Open for the residual the check cannot reach: a key supplied as a non-literal expression would not be enumerated. Pick up if any caller ever passes a computed key, or when a fifth client is added | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-161 | `identityVerified` in `lib/data/tenant.ts` is a module-scoped promise memo, so `assertRestrictedIdentity` verifies the RLS role of the FIRST connection the process opens and trusts every later one for the process's lifetime | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Surfaced by Task 6's round-4 implementer as a second-order consequence of D-157 (the lazy Proxy rebuilding a client per request), then re-analysed by the scoped re-review, which sharpened it: while D-157 was live, every per-request pool still authenticated from the same static `APP_DATABASE_URL`, so the added exposure was not "later clients have unknown identity" but "the number of never-directly-checked connections grew without bound". Restoring the singleton reverts the system to its pre-D-157 behaviour, which is this memo's original and apparently deliberate design. Recorded as its own row rather than left as a paragraph inside D-157's status cell, because that is the laundered-deferral shape AGENTS.md §6 names — the two sibling findings from the same pass each got a row (D-159, D-160) and this one did not, and an inconsistency in what gets registered is exactly what makes a register stop being an audit surface. **What is NOT claimed:** that this is a live defect today. `assertRestrictedIdentity` checks a role that is fixed by an environment variable and cannot change under a running process, so re-verifying per connection would catch only a misconfiguration that a restart would equally reveal | A change that makes the app role dynamic per connection or per tenant (connection-per-tenant, role switching, or PgBouncer-style pooling where a physical connection's role is not guaranteed stable), any of which breaks the assumption this memo rests on. Also re-examine if `hmrSingleton`'s module-scope caching is ever replaced by a process-wide registry, since the two memos would then have different lifetimes | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-162 | `evidence.filename` has no length or content bound at upload time -- `file.name` (fully attacker/client-controlled) is stored verbatim | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found during Task 7's fresh C1 senior-security pass while threat-modeling the DOWNLOAD path, then verified by reading rather than assumed: `grep -n "filename" lib/evidence/inspect.ts lib/data/evidence.ts lib/validate.ts app/api/v1/orgs/[slug]/assessments/[id]/evidence/route.ts` shows `filename: file.name` written straight into `createEvidence`'s input with no length cap and no character restriction anywhere on the upload path (Task 6). NOT a vulnerability in the download route Task 7 built: O-8's RFC 6266 escaping (ascii fallback + `filename*`) is correctness-preserving regardless of input length, so an unbounded filename cannot header-inject or break quoting merely by being long. The residual risk is robustness/hygiene, not injection -- an extreme filename could bloat the `evidence` row, or (more concretely) produce a `Content-Disposition` value long enough to exceed a proxy's or browser's own header-size limit, which would make a legitimately-uploaded file's download fail rather than expose anything. Out of Task 7's file scope to fix (the upload route is Task 6's, already merged and reviewed; touching it now would be the AGENTS.md §2 "file the brief did not name" trigger) | Task 10 (the attach UI, which needs a client-side length affordance regardless of any server-side cap), or the first observed Content-Disposition/proxy failure on a real long filename, or whoever next touches the upload route's validation | Phase 1d | Open **Re-targeted 2026-08-09 at the Phase 1c phase-exit register reconciliation.** A related but narrower fix landed: `components/evidence/EvidenceAttach.tsx`'s `FILENAME_STYLE` (added during Task 10) prevents an overlong filename from breaking the list's layout via CSS `overflowWrap`/`wordBreak` -- its own comment names the exact defect this row opened as its motivation. That is a display-robustness fix, not the length/content bound this row actually asks for: no upload-time validation exists anywhere (`filename` flows through `lib/evidence/inspect.ts`, `lib/data/evidence.ts` and the upload route unchecked), so the two consequences the row names -- an oversized DB row, and a `Content-Disposition` header long enough to exceed a proxy's limit -- remain fully possible. Precise progress, not closure. |

Also re-examined and reworded the drift banner copy (`ReportSummary.tsx`/`ReportPdf.tsx`, now sharing one `driftBannerText()` in `lib/report/provenance.ts`), which previously claimed "not been recomputed against the newer content" -- true only for an assessment whose completion was itself gated on drift, which cannot be verified after the fact for any assessment that predates this fix. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean both times. I am extending your scope to close it"). `respondToAssessment` now calls `resolveFramework` inside its existing `withOrg` transaction (proven by the file's own pre-existing `withOrgCallCount` assertion, reused rather than duplicated) and returns `{ kind: 'drifted' }`, distinct from `{ kind: 'completed' }`; the PATCH route maps it to its own 409 message. Verified live: `npx vitest run __tests__/integration/assessments.test.ts` — 11/11 passing, including a real HTTP-level round trip through the actual route handler (not just the lib function).
