Fix round 1 declined logging partly on the reasoning that switching the PATCH route from `requireOrgContext` to `requireOrgContextWithIdentity` (needed for `identity.userId` in a log call) would be "a bigger structural change than the coordinator's four explicit requirements asked for." FALSE, confirmed by reading `lib/auth/context.ts:82-85`: `requireOrgContext` is a two-line wrapper -- `const { ctx } = await requireOrgContextWithIdentity(slug, action); return ctx;` -- that already calls the identity-returning function internally and discards the identity half. Switching call sites is a one-line change with zero additional auth or database work, not a structural one. **The decision to decline stands, but now on its remaining true reason alone**: both routes already log SOME outcomes inconsistently -- the PATCH route logged NONE of its rejections before this round; the complete route logs only its success case (`ASSESSMENT_COMPLETED`), never `not_found`/`no_responses`/`no_stages`. Adding `logSecurityEvent` to ONLY the new `'drifted'` branches in each route would not resolve that inconsistency, it would add a third, arbitrarily-chosen logged case to an already-inconsistent set, with no principled reason drift specifically earns an audit trail while `not_found`/`no_stages` do not | Whoever next does a deliberate security-observability pass over these two routes: add `logSecurityEvent` for EVERY rejection kind in BOTH routes at once (`not_found`, `completed`/`already_completed` where applicable, `no_responses`, `no_stages`, `drifted`), matching the fully-logged pattern the evidence upload/download routes already establish, rather than continuing to add coverage piecemeal per new branch | Phase 1d or 2 | Open **Re-targeted 2026-08-09 at the Phase 1c phase-exit register reconciliation.** Confirmed both asymmetries are unchanged: the PATCH `assessments/[id]` route has zero `logSecurityEvent` calls (grep), and the complete route logs only `ASSESSMENT_COMPLETED` on success, never its rejection branches. No task after this row was opened did the "all rejections, both routes, at once" pass it asks for. |
| D-169 | This repo has no DOM-rendering test infrastructure -- no jsdom, no `@testing-library/react`, no `react-test-renderer`. Verified: `package.json` names none of them, and `vitest.config.ts` runs every suite under `environment: 'node'` | PARKED | 2026-08-09 | Not a decision either task made -- Task 9 (`report-provenance.test.tsx`) and Task 10 (`EvidenceAttach.tsx`/`evidence-attach.test.tsx`) each independently hit this pre-existing constraint and shaped a design around it silently, with no register row until Task 10's fix-round reviewer grepped the register (168 rows at the time) and found zero mentions of jsdom -- the D-063/D-068 shape §6 exists to catch: a triggered constraint with no trace. The constraint forces two consequences: (1) a component under test must stay hookless so it can be called directly as a plain function and its returned, unrendered element tree walked structurally -- calling a hook-bearing component outside a real React render throws "Invalid hook call" -- so escaping/accessibility/attribute proofs in this codebase are structural (is a value a scalar string at `.props.children`, is `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` absent from source) rather than DOM-observed (`.innerHTML`, a computed ARIA role, actual focus/keyboard behaviour); (2) stateful (`'use client'`, hook-bearing) components have no unit-test coverage at all -- `AssessmentPageClient.tsx` is the concrete instance, verified only via `tsc --noEmit` and the e2e Playwright suite, which is thorough but requires a live dev server and database and is far slower per iteration than a unit test | A human partner decision on whether to add jsdom plus `@testing-library/react` (or an equivalent) as a dev dependency -- explicitly NOT proposed or decided here, that is a dependency-addition decision the coordinator owns, matching how D-146 (no CI) was also routed to the human partner rather than installed unilaterally. Would unblock: real DOM-rendered escaping proofs instead of structural ones; computed-ARIA-role assertions instead of inferring roles from element tag names; simulated click/keyboard/focus interaction tests without a full Playwright browser; and unit coverage for stateful client components, which today have none | Phase 1d (pending human partner decision) | Open **Re-targeted 2026-08-09 at the Phase 1c phase-exit register reconciliation. Status preserved, not resolved unilaterally**, on the same footing as D-146 -- this row's own text explicitly analogizes itself to D-146 ("matching how D-146... was also routed to the human partner rather than installed unilaterally"). Confirmed unchanged: `package.json` names no `jsdom`, `@testing-library/react` or `react-test-renderer`; `vitest.config.ts:6` still sets `environment: 'node'`. |
| D-170 | `evidenceStale` (fix round 2) is tracked ONLY for the question whose own mutation triggered a failed confirmation re-fetch -- a sibling question that already had non-empty evidence, was NOT the one just mutated, gets no stale signal at all even though the SAME global `fetchEvidence()` failure means its currently-displayed list is equally unconfirmed | RISK | 2026-08-09 | A conscious scope boundary, not an oversight: the coordinator's round-2 fix-round instructions named the specific silent case to close (a just-mutated, non-empty question showing no signal at all) and explicitly flagged the BROADER shared-flag characteristic (`evidenceListError`/`evidenceLoading` are single flags covering every question on the page, noted by the round-1 re-reviewer) as pre-existing and out of scope -- "I am not asking you to restructure it." Extending `evidenceStale` to every non-empty question whenever the shared `evidenceListError` flag is true would also raise a design question round 2 was not scoped to answer: should a question NOBODY touched this session show a warning purely because an unrelated mutation's re-fetch failed, and if so with what wording (it did not "just get saved", so round 2's chosen copy would be wrong for it)? Deciding that is exactly the sharing-architecture question already named out of scope, not a small addition | Whenever `evidenceListError`/`evidenceLoading`'s shared-across-every-question characteristic is addressed architecturally (its own trigger is unnamed anywhere yet) -- extend the same per-question staleness signal to every question with non-empty items, not only the one whose own mutation triggered the failure, with wording appropriate to "this may be stale" rather than "your change was saved but..." | Phase 1d or later, no trigger fired yet | Open **Re-targeted 2026-08-09 at the Phase 1c phase-exit register reconciliation.** This row's own text says its trigger is "unnamed anywhere yet" -- that remains true; nothing in the shared `evidenceListError`/`evidenceLoading` architecture was revisited after Task 10 closed. No specific gate to name, so carried forward without a firm phase commitment beyond "not before Phase 1d." |
| D-171 | `GET /api/users/me/export` (O-12, Task 11) includes evidence METADATA the caller uploaded, never the underlying bytes -- `task-11-brief.md`'s own checklist step said "metadata and bytes, base64-encoded"; implemented the other way | DEFERRAL | 2026-08-09 | **CORRECTED, task review fix round 1 (2026-08-09) -- read this before the reasoning below.** The row as first written cited "the deeper obligation section" of the brief and called the size-cost framing "O-12's own reasoning" (quoting a phrase, "false statement about what the system holds"). Neither exists in any repository document: `task-11-brief.md` is 4 checklist steps and nothing else (verified: `wc -l` = 22, re-read in full), and the design doc's actual O-12 line (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-phase1c-evidence-and-pinning-design.md:461`) is one sentence -- "includes evidence -- export and delete stay symmetric" -- with no elaboration on bytes at all. Both the "decide deliberately" framing and the "false statement" phrase came from the coordinator's DISPATCH MESSAGE for this task, given directly to the implementer in conversation, not from anything checked into the repository -- a claim generated to explain a choice, caught only because a reviewer went looking for the citation and could not find it (AGENTS.md §5's highest-danger class: it arrived already feeling like an observation). Fixed here rather than silently rewritten, per the same rule this register exists to enforce. The engineering reasoning immediately below (`NextResponse.json`, `MAX_BYTES`, `EVIDENCE_LIST_LIMIT`) was independently verified against this codebase's and Next.js's own source and is UNCHANGED; only the two false attributions are removed, and two real citations the reviewer found are added in their place. Engineering reasoning (unchanged, grounded in code read directly): this route has no pagination and no streaming. `NextResponse.json` was checked against Next.js's own source (`node_modules/next/dist/server/web/spec-extension/response.js`, `static json()`) rather than assumed: it calls the standard `Response.json()`, which serializes the whole body before constructing the Response -- not a streaming API. A prolific uploader's evidence, base64-encoded (~4/3 inflation on top of the raw bytes, `MAX_BYTES` = 10 MiB per file, `lib/evidence/inspect.ts`), could turn one GET into a multi-hundred-MB synchronous JSON build with no cap -- the identical unbounded-growth shape `EVIDENCE_LIST_LIMIT` (`lib/data/evidence.ts`) already exists to bound for plain metadata, except bytes make the worst case categorically larger. Silently CAPPING an export the way `EVIDENCE_LIST_LIMIT` caps a browsing list is not an available mitigation here: an export that silently drops rows past a cap would itself misstate what the system holds. Metadata alone is still a true, complete account of WHAT the user uploaded (filename, size, hash, timestamp, which assessment/org) -- the same way this route has never restated a ConsentRecord's underlying policy text, only the record that consent happened. Real grounding for the decision (found by the reviewer, both pre-dating this task): design doc §4.3 (`...design.md:~306`) already specifies the identical philosophy for the PDF surface -- the evidence section there is "an evidence **manifest** -- filename, SHA-256, uploader, date -- and **not** the bytes... a hash is stronger provenance than an embedded copy, and the PDF stays bounded." Design doc §1 decision #5 (`...design.md:~45`, the human partner's own ruling) goes further: "On user erasure, evidence **bytes are kept**, attribution scrubbed." Verified against the actual code, not just the decision table: `scrubUserOnDeactivation` (`lib/data/identity.ts:363-380`) updates only the `User` row and deletes only `ConsentRecord` rows -- it contains no statement touching `Evidence` at all. A further check while fixing this row (same standard: verify, do not extend the claim past what was checked) found no ORGANIZATION-deletion path exists in production either -- `org:delete` is declared in `lib/authz/policy.ts`'s grants but has no route handler anywhere in `app/`, and `Organization.deletedAt` is written by no code in `app/` or `lib/`, only ever read as a filter. So the export/delete symmetry O-12 exists for is substantially already satisfied by metadata alone, independent of this task's own choice: no account or organization lifecycle event destroys evidence bytes at all, by construction. The only path that irrecoverably destroys them today is the deliberate, single-item `DELETE .../orgs/[slug]/evidence/[evidenceId]` (gated to owner/admin/assessor, refused on a completed assessment, O-24) -- never account deletion, never (today) org deletion. The residual this decision leaves is narrow and real: a user who treats the export JSON as a complete offline backup, then deliberately deletes an individual item without having downloaded its bytes first through the per-item download route, loses those bytes | A real user requests the underlying bytes through this route specifically (as opposed to the existing per-item download route), or before any deployment where "one export call" is expected to be a complete backup rather than an index -- at that point this needs a genuine streaming or paginated/bulk-job design, not a larger in-memory response | Phase 2, gated on D-018 | Open **Re-targeted 2026-08-09 at the Phase 1c phase-exit register reconciliation.** Neither trigger condition has fired -- no real user has requested the underlying bytes through this route, and D-018 hosting remains Parked so there is no deployment for "one export call as a complete backup" to matter against. The row's reasoning was itself corrected once already this phase (task review fix round 1, 2026-08-09) and re-verified here: `app/api/users/me/export/route.ts` still returns evidence metadata only, no byte content. |
| D-172 | `org:delete` is granted to `owner` but consulted by no route, and `Organization.deletedAt` is read as a filter in three places and written by nothing — an orphaned guard plus a read with no writer | BUG | 2026-08-09 | Found incidentally by Task 11's implementer while verifying that no account or organisation lifecycle event destroys evidence bytes, and confirmed by the controller: `can('owner','org:delete')` returns true, `grep -rn "org:delete" app/ lib/authz/routeActions.ts` returns zero hits, and `deletedAt` appears three times as `deletedAt: null` in queries with zero assignments anywhere in `lib/` or `app/`. **This is exactly the pair of shapes `__tests__/integration/trigger-enumeration.test.ts` was built to catch** — the same class as `bumpSessionEpoch`, which shipped with no production caller, and `mustChangePassword`, which was read after its writer was removed (D-136's second proposed check is read/write pairing, and this is a textbook instance). Pre-existing rather than introduced by Plan 1c; surfaced now because Task 11 extended that very test with nine new entries and the implementer was reasoning about lifecycle paths. **What it means today:** organisation deletion does not exist as a feature. The grant and the column are both scaffolding for it, and the soft-delete filter silently protects against a state nothing can produce. Nothing is broken and nothing leaks — an owner who tries to delete an org simply finds no way to. The risk is that the filter reads as evidence the feature exists, so a future reader may assume soft-delete works and build on it | Whichever comes first: a decision to build organisation deletion (at which point the grant, the column and the filter all become live and need their own threat pass, since deleting an org means deciding what happens to its assessments, evidence bytes and members), or the next pass over `trigger-enumeration.test.ts`, which should gain an entry pinning that `org:delete` has a route caller — so this cannot silently persist a third phase. Deliberately NOT fixed by deleting the grant: the column and filter would still be orphaned, and removing a declared capability is a product decision rather than a cleanup | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-173 | The sidebar shows a user's PLATFORM role under their name with no label, so an organisation owner reads "Assessor" on every screen | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found at Task 12's live C5 walk by looking at a screenshot, not by any assertion — no test failed and none could, since the label is correct for the value it renders. `app/(authenticated)/layout.tsx:14` passes `identity.platformRole` to `Sidebar`, which renders it bare at `components/layout/Sidebar.tsx:118`. Verified against the database: the fixture owner has `users.role = 'assessor'` and `memberships.role = 'owner'` — two different role systems, both real. The component's own doc comment (lines 16-22) explains why its admin-link gate deliberately uses the PLATFORM role rather than an arbitrary org's role, and that reasoning is sound and should not be undone. **The gate is right; the unlabelled display is the problem.** In a product where the org role governs essentially everything a user does — every route action, every rendered control, the entire 726-cell permission matrix — showing the platform role under the user's name, with no indication of which kind of role it is, tells an owner they are an assessor. The likeliest harm is not technical: a pilot participant who reads "Assessor" while holding owner powers has been given a false model of their own authority, in a governance product whose whole subject is knowing who is accountable for what | Task 10's own D-169 (no DOM test infrastructure) means no unit test can pin sidebar rendering today, so pick this up with whichever comes first: the first UI pass after a real pilot participant is confused by it, or any work that adds an org-role indicator to the chrome. Cheapest honest fix is a label rather than a value change — "Platform: Assessor" plus the org role where the org switcher already lives — since changing WHICH role is shown would break the deliberate platform-role gate documented in the same file | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-174 | `Evidence.uploadedById` is nullable, and the PDF manifest's 'Unknown' fallback branch exists, for an erasure behaviour nothing implements and nothing can | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Residual of the C6 whole-branch review finding F2, recorded rather than tidied away. Spec §6 O-24 required that "a user scrub nulls `uploadedById` while leaving the row and its bytes intact". That was never implementable: `makrai_app` holds `SELECT, INSERT, DELETE` on `evidence` and deliberately NO `UPDATE` (`prisma/migrations/20260808140048_evidence_tables/migration.sql:129-134`, "No UPDATE: evidence is uploaded or deleted, never edited"), so no code path can change any column on an existing evidence row. Verified rather than inferred: `scrubUserOnDeactivation` (`lib/data/identity.ts:363-381`) updates only the `users` row and deletes `consent_records`; a case-insensitive grep for "scrub" across `app/ lib/ prisma/` returns exactly the deactivation path and the two now-corrected PDF-route comments; `CreateEvidenceInput.uploadedById` is typed as a plain string rather than nullable, so the write path cannot produce a null either. The column's `ON DELETE SET NULL` is equally latent -- `identityDb` exposes no delete operation at all and nothing ever deletes a `User`. **Nothing is broken and no PII leaks:** erasure scrubs `users.name` to 'Deleted user', which is what `lookupUserNames` returns and what the manifest renders, so §1 decision #5 (bytes kept, attribution scrubbed) is satisfied by a different mechanism than the spec named. Spec O-24 and §5.2 are corrected in place, and the two production comments asserting the scrub are fixed. What is left is a nullable column and a dead fallback branch that together describe a capability the product does not have -- the same read-as-evidence-of-a-feature hazard D-106 and D-172 record for their own columns. **Deliberately NOT resolved by making the column NOT NULL now:** that is a migration on a table holding user bytes, and the right moment to decide whether erasure should null attribution at the row level is when someone decides whether evidence should be editable at all | Whichever comes first: a real erasure requirement that the `users`-row scrub does not satisfy (e.g. a DPO ruling that attribution must be severed at the evidence row, which would need an `UPDATE` grant and a re-run of the O-1/O-5 grant threat model), or the first migration that touches `evidence` for another reason -- at which point either make `uploadedById` NOT NULL and delete the dead branch, or write down why it stays nullable | Phase 2 | Open |
| D-175 | A completed report's SCORES are frozen but its FINDINGS are not -- the HTML report page rebuilds gaps, areas to strengthen, strengths and not-yet-assessed from deployed content on every view | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Residual of C6 finding F3, which was about the drift banner's copy; this row is about the behaviour the copy had to be made honest about. `components/report/useFindingsData.ts` statically imports `data/questionBank.json` and `data/assessmentAreas.json` (lines 4-5) and recomputes `gaps`/`attentions`/`strengths`/`notAssessed` plus the per-principle question lists inside a `useMemo` keyed on `report`/`engineState` only (lines 236-241) -- never on the pin. `Assessment.reportData` caches `overallScore`, `principleScores`, `stageScores` and `completedStages` and nothing else, so each principle's SCORE is as-recorded while the QUESTIONS listed under it, and the three counts `ReportSummary` renders, are today's. Two auditors opening the same completed assessment either side of a content edit can therefore see the same percentages over a different list of findings, with no record of which they saw. **Not fixed in this wave, deliberately.** Making the claim true rather than accurate means snapshotting the question/area content into `reportData` at completion -- a data-model change plus a backfill decision for every existing completed row, and it overlaps directly with Plan 2a's framework-as-data work (D-137, D-138), which will decide whether content lives in rows at all. Choosing the shape now would be exactly the foreclosure D-137 binds Plan 1c against. What 1c does instead is disclose it: the drift banner's live-findings variant now states in terms that the findings are rebuilt from today's content and can differ from what was recorded | Plan 2a design, where the framework content model is settled -- decide there whether a completed assessment snapshots the content it was answered against, or whether the report is defined as scores-plus-live-commentary and says so on every view rather than only under drift | Phase 2a | Open |
| D-176 | `evidence.remediationItemId` is a SINGLE-column FK to a tenant table, so an org's evidence can reference another org's remediation item and be cascade-deleted by it | RISK | 2026-08-09 | C6 whole-branch review, F4. `prisma/migrations/20260808140048_evidence_tables/migration.sql:61-62` declares `remediationItemId TEXT REFERENCES remediation_items(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` -- plain `id`, not the `(orgId, id)` composite the sibling `evidence_assessment_fkey` two lines below uses, because `remediation_items` carries no `UNIQUE(orgId, id)` for a composite FK to target. FK checks run with the REFERENCED table's rights, not the inserting role's, so RLS does not constrain which remediation item a row may point at. Proven live at the review, not inferred: an org-A evidence row was made to reference an org-B remediation item, and org B deleting its own item cascaded org A's evidence away. **Not reachable today**, which is why this is a RISK and not a BUG: the upload route's O-6 confinement check (`app/api/v1/orgs/[slug]/assessments/[id]/evidence/route.ts`) looks the target up through the tenant-scoped `tx`, so a foreign id 404s before any insert; and `remediation_items` has no production writer at all (D-117), so a second org's items cannot exist to be pointed at through the product. Both of those are application-level, and the whole point of the composite-FK pattern used twice already in this schema (`evidence_assessment_fkey`, `evidence_blobs`) is that tenant integrity should not depend on an application check remembering to run. The fix is known and small: `UNIQUE(orgId, id)` on `remediation_items`, then re-declare this FK over `(orgId, remediationItemId)` | D-117 being picked up -- the moment `remediation_items` gains a production writer, this stops being unreachable and becomes a live cross-tenant integrity hole. Plan 2's remediation workflow is that moment. Fix it in the SAME change that adds the writer, not after: adding the writer first means the window is open for however long the follow-up takes | Phase 2 -- with D-117 | Open |
| D-177 | `Invitation.status` can never become `expired` or `revoked`, so a mis-typed or regretted invitation cannot be withdrawn | BUG | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep and re-verified before recording. `InvitationStatus` declares four values; grepping `app/` and `lib/` for writes of the `expired` or `revoked` values returns nothing, and the only production write to the column is `status: 'accepted'` in `lib/data/preauth.ts:324`. There is no revoke route (`lib/authz/routeActions.ts` declares no such action), no expiry sweep (no scheduled job exists in this codebase at all), and no screen listing pending invitations -- `MembersManager.tsx` renders members, not invitations. `expiresAt` IS enforced at redemption time, so an expired token cannot be used; what is missing is the ability to make that state visible or to end an invitation early. **Consequence, which is larger than the orphan:** an invitation sent to the wrong address is irrevocable for its whole validity window, and the org has no surface that even shows it exists. For a governance product whose subject is knowing who has access to what, an unlistable, unrevocable grant-of-access is the wrong default. Same class as D-106 and D-172 -- a declared state machine the product cannot drive -- but unlike those two this one has a user-visible consequence today | The first pilot org that sends a real invitation -- or sooner, whichever task next opens the members/settings surface for another reason. The minimum honest fix is a pending-invitations list plus a revoke action writing the `revoked` status; the expiry sweep can stay unbuilt as long as redemption keeps checking `expiresAt` | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-178 | `ConsentRecord.version` is never written, so every consent record claims version "1.0" regardless of which policy text the user actually saw | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep, verified in full. `prisma/schema.prisma:249` declares `version String @default("1.0")`; `consentRecordsData` (`lib/data/preauth.ts:186-196`) is the ONLY producer of consent rows and sets `userId`, `consentType`, `granted` and `ipAddress` -- never `version` -- so every row in the product carries the default. The column IS read: `app/api/users/me/export/route.ts:81` selects it into the user's own data export, so the value is asserted to the data subject. **Why this matters more than an unused column:** a consent record's entire evidentiary purpose is to say WHAT was agreed to, and the only field carrying that is a constant. The moment the terms or privacy text is revised, records made before and after the revision become indistinguishable, and re-consent cannot be demonstrated -- in a product that positions consent handling as an RAI capability. Not fixed here because the fix is not a one-liner in good conscience: it needs a decision about where the version of the policy TEXT lives (today the text is in components, with no version marker anywhere), which is the same missing concept D-008's consent-model work has to settle | Whichever comes first: the first revision to the terms-of-service or privacy text (at that point the pre-revision rows are already ambiguous and the window to fix it cleanly has closed), or D-008 being picked up. The cheap interim measure is a single exported constant for the current policy version, passed through `consentRecordsData`, so the value at least becomes a fact rather than a default | Phase 1d, or with D-008 | Open |
| D-179 | `FrameworkVersion.status` has no production writer, so `deprecated` is unreachable and the `published` filter is inert | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep. The column is READ as a gate -- `resolvePublishedVersion` (`lib/data/framework.ts`) filters on `status: 'published'` on every assessment creation -- and written by nothing in `app/` or `lib/`: the only writes anywhere are the `20260807102031_framework_registry_and_pin` migration's single `INSERT` and test fixtures. So the filter can only ever match, and a version cannot be retired. That is harmless today (one row exists) and becomes wrong the moment a second version is registered and someone needs to stop new assessments pinning to the old one. **Distinct from D-147 and cross-referenced rather than merged:** D-147 covers the AUTHORING dependency -- that bumping `data/assessmentAreas.json`'s `meta.version` without hand-writing a paired migration makes every `POST .../assessments` throw -- which is the same registry gap seen from the content author's side and already has its own trigger. This row is the narrower structural fact that the lifecycle column itself is undrivable. Both close together in Plan 2a; kept separate because D-147's trigger (a content-version bump) can fire without anyone caring about deprecation, and vice versa | Plan 2a design, where publishing a framework version is designed as a single operation covering content, hash, registry row and status together -- the same trigger D-147 carries. Whichever of the two is picked up first should close both, and should say so | Phase 2a | Open |
| D-180 | `AUTH_LOGOUT` is declared in the `SecurityEvent` union and emitted by nothing, so every login is logged and no logout is | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep. Grepping the whole repo for the quoted `AUTH_LOGOUT` literal returns exactly one hit, the declaration at `lib/security-logger.ts:12`. Logout is entirely client-side `signOut()` from `next-auth/react` (three call sites: `components/layout/Sidebar.tsx:121`, `AcceptInvitationClient.tsx:51`, `change-password/page.tsx:33`), which posts to NextAuth's own handler -- code this project does not own and does not currently hook. `AUTH_LOGIN_SUCCESS` and `AUTH_LOGOUT_EVERYWHERE` both ARE emitted, so the log tells an operator when a session began and when a user revoked all sessions, but never when one ended normally: session timelines cannot be reconstructed from it. The sibling orphan found in the same sweep, `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, WAS fixed in this wave because it was a two-line addition inside code this project owns (`proxy.ts`). This one is not: it needs a NextAuth `events.signOut` callback wired in `lib/auth.ts`, which is a change to the auth configuration and wants its own threat pass on what the callback may read and log. Recorded rather than done, per AGENTS.md §6, instead of being left as a silent gap | Whichever comes first: the first work that touches `lib/auth.ts`'s NextAuth configuration for any reason (add the `events.signOut` hook in that same change), or the first deployment where the structured log is actually consumed by an operator -- at which point missing logout events become an audit finding rather than a latent one | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-181 | `org:update`, `assessment:update` and `assessment:delete` are granted to roles but consulted by no route -- three more orphaned Actions of D-172's shape | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep, same lens that found D-172 (`org:delete`) and the direct sibling of it. Verified the same way: each is declared in `lib/authz/policy.ts`'s `Action` union and appears in `GRANTS`, and a repo-wide grep for each returns hits ONLY in `policy.ts` itself -- no route declares any of them in `lib/authz/routeActions.ts`. So `can()` answers questions nobody asks, and the RBAC matrix's generated cells for these three prove a policy no request consults. **The specific hazard, which is why this is not merely tidy-up:** `assessment:update` is granted to owner, admin AND assessor, one role wider than `assessment:delete` (owner, admin), and Plan 1b's notes record that implementers reach for `assessment:update` by instinct when wiring an assessment-mutating route. The actual action for answering an assessment is `assessment:respond`. An implementer who follows instinct here silently widens who may mutate an assessment, and every existing control passes: the action exists, it is in the matrix, `can()` returns true. Deliberately NOT fixed by deleting the grants -- removing a declared capability is a product decision (the same reasoning D-172 gives), and the deletion would not stop the next implementer choosing the wrong surviving action anyway | The same trigger as D-172, and they should be closed together: the next pass over `__tests__/integration/trigger-enumeration.test.ts`, which should gain an entry asserting that every member of the `Action` union has at least one `ROUTE_ACTIONS` caller -- generated from the union rather than hand-listed, so a fifth orphan cannot appear silently. That check would have found all four of these without anyone looking | Phase 2 -- with D-172 | Open |
| D-182 | The pre-tenancy localStorage layer (`saveState`/`loadState`/`clearState`/`resetAssessment`) is still exported and typed as public API, a ready-made client-side write path for assessment answers into browser storage | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Found by the C6 read/write-pairing sweep. `lib/engine/AssessmentEngine.js:82-113` still defines all four functions against `localStorage` under the key `makai-unified-assessment`, and `lib/engine/AssessmentEngine.d.ts:26-29` declares every one of them as typed public API. A repo-wide grep across `app/` and `components/` finds no caller -- the tenanted flow persists through `PATCH .../assessments/[id]` -- so nothing uses them today. **Why a RISK rather than dead code:** this is not an orphan that merely misleads, it is a working, typed, discoverable API whose whole purpose is to write assessment answers to the browser. An implementer adding offline support or a draft-autosave would find it by autocomplete, and it bypasses tenancy, RLS, the pin and the drift guards entirely -- assessment content for one org sitting in shared browser storage with no org scoping, on a machine this project has already reasoned about being shared (the session `maxAge` decision came from exactly that scenario). It is also untested against any of the current invariants: `__tests__/engine/AssessmentEngine.test.js` clears localStorage between tests but does not exercise these as a persistence path. NOT deleted in this wave: `AssessmentEngine.js` is shared, unbuilt JS with a hand-maintained `.d.ts`, deleting exports from it is a real change with its own test surface, and this wave's scope is the C6 findings | Whichever comes first: any work that touches `lib/engine/AssessmentEngine.js` (delete the four functions and their `.d.ts` declarations in that change -- git history keeps them), or the first proposal for offline or draft-local persistence, which must be designed against tenancy rather than by reaching for these | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-183 | PDF export of an IN-PROGRESS assessment is refused rather than supported, so there is no draft export at all | DEFERRAL | 2026-08-09 | The capability deliberately not built when closing C6 finding F1. That finding was that `GET .../reports/[id]/pdf` rendered a document for a non-completed assessment by computing scores from DEPLOYED content (`generateReportData(assessment.engineState)`, which scores through `AssessmentEngine.js`'s static content imports) and then stamping the PINNED `contentHash` on it -- with the drift banner suppressed by construction, since `isFrameworkDrifted` returns false for any status but `completed`. Two shapes were open: gate the route on status, or make the drift determination account for a freshly-computed report. Gating was chosen and the reasoning is recorded in the route's own comment: a banner can only DISCLOSE a false provenance line, never make it true, and the line would still have claimed the pinned content for numbers computed from other content. The alternative that would genuinely support a draft export is to compute it against the PINNED content -- which requires the pinned content to be retrievable, i.e. Plan 2a's framework-as-data work (D-137). **Nothing in the product lost a flow:** the HTML report page already redirected a non-completed assessment away (`report/page.tsx:61-64`), so only a hand-typed URL reached this, and what it returned was wrong. The 409 says plainly that the assessment must be completed first | A user asking for a draft or interim export -- realistically a pilot team wanting to circulate progress before completion. Build it only once pinned content is retrievable (D-137 and Plan 2a), so the export can be computed against the version the assessment is pinned to and the provenance line stays true; do NOT reinstate it by computing against deployed content | Phase 2a | Open |
| D-184 | `npm run lint` exits 1 on this branch -- one `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error -- and no register row recorded it, so `npm run verify` is red at the Phase 1c merge decision | BUG | 2026-08-09 | **CORRECTED 2026-08-10, before the Phase 1c merge decision, by the agent that fixed the error.** This row's own text called the error "Pre-existing and NOT introduced by this wave" -- that is wrong, and the stash test behind it only ever had the power to answer a narrower question than the one this row asked. Re-checked directly, against the actual question of whether the BRANCH (not just the fix wave) introduced it: `npm run lint` exits 0 at the branch's merge-base with `main` (`8d1539a`, via `git merge-base HEAD main`) and exits 1 at HEAD (`19bbd17`), with the identical single `AssessmentPageClient.tsx` error both times. The branch introduced this, full stop. Bisected further by checking out each Task 10 commit and running `npx eslint` fresh at each: exit 0 at `88707e3` (Task 10's own feat commit -- `fetchEvidence` was still an effect-local `async function`, the same shape `fetchAssessment` two hooks above it still uses today) and exit 1 starting at `9fc9aa3` (Task 10 round 1, the silent-failure-hunter fix, D-169) -- the exact commit that extracted `fetchEvidence` out of the effect into a shared `useCallback` so `handleEvidenceUpload`/`handleEvidenceDelete` could call it too. **Why the earlier stash test could not have caught this:** it compared HEAD (`19bbd17`) against `3114fb7` with the fix wave's own changes stashed -- but `3114fb7` is already three commits downstream of every Task 10 commit (`88707e3`, `9fc9aa3`, `92d972c` all precede it in `git log --oneline main..HEAD`). Stashing the fix wave and re-testing against `3114fb7` can only ever show whether the FIX WAVE introduced the error; it structurally cannot see anything that happened earlier in the branch, because the comparison baseline never reached back that far. "The fix wave did not introduce it" and "it is pre-existing" are different claims, and the row asserted the second on evidence that only supported the first -- a self-inflicted regression called pre-existing sends the next reader looking in the wrong place and quietly lowers the bar for merging it. **FIXED, same pass as this correction.** The mount effect (`useEffect(() => { fetchEvidence(); }, [fetchEvidence]);`, lines 290-292) called the shared `useCallback` bare and synchronously; the rule flags that because it can prove the callee sets state, but its analysis does not follow the same call through a NEW nested function boundary -- confirmed empirically with an isolated, never-committed probe file testing seven shapes: `void fn()` and `fn().catch(...)` stayed flagged, while an IIFE, a local sync wrapper, a local async wrapper, `queueMicrotask`, and `setTimeout` all passed. Fixed by wrapping the call in a local `function run() { fetchEvidence(); } run();` inside the effect. Written `async`/`await` at first (matching `fetchAssessment`'s own effect two hooks above, which uses that shape for its OWN, different reason -- it contains its own `await`s); a `simplify` pass afterward flagged the `async`/`await` on THIS wrapper as doing nothing observable, since `fetchEvidence`'s own try/catch/finally never lets its promise reject and `run()`'s return value is discarded either way -- dropped in favour of the plain synchronous form, re-verified (lint, the regression test's red/green proof below, full `vitest`/`tsc`, and one more live `playwright test` run, all re-confirmed clean on the final shape). `fetchEvidence` itself is untouched: same `isMountedRef.current`-guarded setState calls, same `Promise<boolean>` contract `mutateThenConfirm`'s callers rely on to distinguish `mutate-failed` from `confirm-failed`, same single `setEvidenceStale({})` clearing every question's flag on success in the one place that knows the whole list is fresh, same `finally` clearing `evidenceLoading`. Not a suppression -- the rule is not disabled anywhere, only the mount effect's call shape changed. Verified: `npm run lint` exit 0 (was 1); `npx vitest run __tests__/unit/mutate-then-confirm.test.ts` 4/4 (the mutate-failed/confirm-failed distinction this fix must not disturb); `npx vitest run __tests__/unit/evidence-attach.test.tsx` 39/39; full `npx vitest run` 1566/1566 across 49 files; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Live, via `npx playwright test` against the real running app: `e2e/evidence-lifecycle.spec.ts`'s Task 12 walk uploads evidence over the real API, then does a fresh `page.goto()` and asserts the filename renders -- exercising exactly this mount fetch -- and it passed in all 3 post-fix runs. Regression-pinned mechanically, per `superpowers:test-driven-development`'s non-vacuity requirement: `__tests__/lint/assessment-page-client.test.ts` shells out to the real `eslint` against this exact file (same technique `__tests__/lint/effective-config.test.ts` already uses, rather than a hand-rolled proxy for a React-Compiler-based static-analysis rule) and asserts zero errors -- this repo has no DOM test infrastructure to render the component and observe its effect directly (D-169), so this is the closest available pin. Written after the fix rather than before it (the fix was already applied and verified via the existing tests above when this test was added), so proven non-vacuous by hand instead: reverted the effect to the bare `fetchEvidence();` shape, re-ran the new test, watched it fail with the identical `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error at the identical location; restored the fix, re-ran, watched it pass; full suite re-confirmed green after (`npm run lint` 0, `npx vitest run` 1567/1567 across 50 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean). Whether this was ALSO D-185's root cause was tested directly (6 runs before this fix, 3 after, all recorded there); the verdict on that row is evidence against a shared root -- D-185 stays open on its own | Closed by the fix recorded above. If `AssessmentPageClient.tsx`'s evidence mount effect is touched again, keep the local-wrapper indirection (or an equally non-suppressing replacement) rather than reverting to a bare call -- `npm run lint` will catch a regression either way, now that this row explains why the wrapper exists | Phase 1c merge decision -- met | Closed-done |
| D-185 | `e2e/assessment-flow.spec.ts` fails intermittently -- a radio stays unchecked after its label is clicked -- so `npx playwright test` is red at the Phase 1c merge decision | BUG | 2026-08-09 **ROOT CAUSE FOUND, 2026-08-10 (Task 7). This row's cause is no longer unknown.** Step 1 (the brief's cheapest experiment) ran first and came back CLEAN but UNDER-POWERED, and the control arm is what made that readable. Arm A -- `{ timeout: 15000 }` added to `:218`, the test temporarily un-`fixme`d, ten full-suite runs (`npx playwright test`, 6 workers) -- gave 10/10 clean, `49 passed`, 70-74s per run. Under this row's own ~1-in-3 figure P(10 clean) = (2/3)^10 = 1.7%, and an exact one-sided 95% bound from 0/10 puts the failure rate at <= 25.9% -- so that sweep rules out 1-in-3 and NOTHING ELSE. Arm B, the control -- identical tree, same machine, same session, `:218` back at the 5s default -- reproduced this row's exact symptom on run 3 of 10: `expect(locator).toBeChecked() failed / Locator: locator('.question-block').first().locator('input').first() / Expected: checked / Received: unchecked / Timeout: 5000ms / 14 x locator resolved to <input value="0" type="radio" name="Q-PP-01"/> - unexpected value "unchecked"`, in a 70s run with no load anomaly. At that 1-in-10 rate 10 clean runs has probability 31%, i.e. arm A is not evidence the timeout helps. (A third arm using 8 synthetic CPU hogs was ABANDONED and is reported as no data: the load starved the fixture build itself -- `auth.setup.ts:88` `page.fill('#email')` hit the 30s timeout, `1 failed / 48 did not run`, four runs running -- so it measured the fixture, not the flake.) **THE MECHANISM, established by instrumenting a NATURAL failure and then reproducing it deterministically, harness-only (`page.addInitScript` + `page.route`), with no file under `app/`, `lib/` or `components/` touched.** `AssessmentPageClient.tsx:177-216`'s mount effect calls `setEngineState(data.engineState)` at **`:205`** unconditionally -- no request-ordering or staleness guard, no 'the user has already edited' guard, and not even the `isMountedRef.current` check its own sibling `setLoading` carries at `:210`. The page issues that GET MORE THAN ONCE: React StrictMode is ON for the App Router (`node_modules/next/dist/build/define-env.js:142-144` -- `__NEXT_STRICT_MODE_APP` is `true` whenever `reactStrictMode` is unset, and `next.config.ts` never sets it), so the dev double-mount runs the effect again. Measured, not assumed: a `page.route` counter logged `assessment GETs for one page load = 2` on a quiet run. The instrumented natural failure (hunt run 2 of 20, failing question `Q-PP-02` -- a FOURTH distinct block after Q-PP-01, 03 and 06) shows **four** concurrent GETs of `/api/v1/orgs/.../assessments/<id>` issued at t=5413/5415/5417/5419ms and resolving at t=6434/6463/6777/**7046**; the two answers were recorded at t=6954 (`Q-PP-01[0]` change, checked=true) and t=7042 (`Q-PP-02[0]` change, checked=true); and **19ms after the last, late response landed, at t=7065, the capture records exactly two `checked` property writes, both `true -> false`, on the two value-`0` radios that had just been answered** -- with zero page errors and nothing that ever re-checks them. A constructed probe closes it: fetching the second response early (so its body is the pre-answer server state) and delivering it 6s late makes `toBeChecked({timeout:15000})` PASS right after the click and `input.isChecked()` read `false` afterwards, every time. (The first version of that probe delayed the request's DISPATCH instead of its DELIVERY and the answer SURVIVED, because the 1s autosave had already PATCHed it -- that negative is what pinned the mechanism to response staleness rather than request timing.) **This accounts for every property this row records and that no previous hypothesis did:** the spec alone never fails (no contention, the duplicate response lands in milliseconds); full-suite load is required; the locator always resolves; the value never drifts, because the answer is wiped and nothing will ever restore it -- **so no timeout, at any length, can fix this**, and arm A's 10 clean runs are luck, not a cure; and the failing question MOVES between runs because it is simply whichever block the loop is on when the late response lands. **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, stated rather than papered over.** (a) Why FOUR concurrent GETs rather than two -- the quiet-run count was 2 and the failing run's was 4; that extra doubling is unexplained and is a separate question. (b) Whether the CI surface is exposed AT ALL. Every observation of this symptom, in this row's whole history, is under `npm run dev` (`playwright.config.ts:26` -- `process.env.CI ? 'npm run start' : 'npm run dev'`), while `.github/workflows/ci.yml:346,385` runs the PRODUCTION build. The dev-only trigger is compiled out there: in `node_modules/react-dom/cjs/react-dom-client.production.js` the effect double-invoke functions (`commitDoubleInvokeEffectsInDEV`, `doubleInvokeEffectsOnFiber`) do not exist at all (0 occurrences vs 3 each in the development build) and `shouldDoubleInvokeUserFnsInHooksDEV` is assigned `!1` at all three of its assignment sites and never set true. A direct runtime confirmation was ATTEMPTED and FAILED for an unrelated local-environment reason, not abandoned: `npm run build` succeeded, then `CI=1 npx playwright test --project=setup` could not log in against `npm run start` -- `page.waitForURL: navigated to http://localhost:3000/api/auth/error`, on all three retries -- because this shell lacks the auth env the CI job sets. So the production-build GET count is UNMEASURED. (c) The missing guard at `:205` is a PRODUCT defect independent of StrictMode: any duplicate, retried or slow assessment response that lands after a user starts answering silently discards their answers, and a real user would see the same thing. No fix was attempted -- this task produces a finding, and the fix (guard `:205` against a stale/superseded response, e.g. an AbortController or a request-sequence ref) is a React data-flow change to a 900-line client component that belongs to whoever picks this row up. Full evidence, the instrumentation fixture, both probe specs and the raw capture are preserved at `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-10-phase1d-i-e2e-trustworthy/task-7-artifacts/`. **THE QUARANTINE WAS NOT LIFTED AND MUST NOT BE READ AS LIFTABLE BY THIS ROW:** `test.fixme` is still at `e2e/assessment-flow.spec.ts:48` (verified after the investigation, `48 passed / 1 skipped` on a clean run), the local un-`fixme` existed only inside Task 7's runs and was never committed, and lifting it is a decision for the controller and the human partner once `:205` is actually fixed. | Found by the C6 fix wave's own C5 verification. PRE-EXISTING and NOT caused by that wave, established by evidence rather than asserted: `npx playwright test` on the fix-wave tree gives 48 passed / 1 failed, and the SAME suite with every fix-wave change stashed (`git stash push --include-untracked`, tree at 3114fb7) gives 48 passed / 1 failed on the SAME test and the SAME assertion. The failure is `answerCurrentModule`'s `await expect(input).toBeChecked()` (`e2e/assessment-flow.spec.ts:218`) after clicking the question block's first label. **Non-deterministic, not a broken selector:** the call log shows the locator resolving correctly to the intended radio every poll, and the block that fails MOVES between runs (Q-PP-01 on two runs, Q-PP-03 on another). Running the spec alone passes (2 passed), so it needs the full-suite load to reproduce. The helper's own doc comment records that it was already hardened once for a related late-render race, so this is the second appearance of that shape. **HYPOTHESIS, offered as a lead and explicitly NOT established:** the likely mechanism is the cascading re-render D-184 records -- `AssessmentPageClient.tsx:290-292` calls a state-setting `fetchEvidence()` synchronously inside a `useEffect`, and `EvidenceAttach` renders inside every question block, so an evidence fetch settling between the click and React's commit re-renders the whole question list and can drop a controlled radio's pending check. That would make D-184 a live defect rather than a lint preference. It has NOT been proven: no instrumentation was added and no fix was attempted, because doing either is a React data-flow change in a 900-line client component that this repo has no DOM test infrastructure to pin (D-169). **What is NOT in doubt:** the PDF-export path this wave changed is fine -- the same spec, including its `download PDF` step, passes end to end on the fix-wave tree when run alone. Also worth recording for whoever picks this up: three back-to-back suite runs with a `git stash`/`pop` in between produced a much WORSE and completely different failure set (4 failed, 20 did not run, `Failed to find Server Action ... older or newer deployment`). That was self-inflicted -- source changing under a reused dev server rotates Server Action ids -- and did not reproduce after `rm -rf .next` and one clean run. Do not chase it as a real defect. **FOURTH DATA POINT, same day, after the fix wave was complete: a clean full run (`rm -rf .next` first) passed 49/49.** So the tally is 2 failures and 1 pass on the fix-wave tree plus 1 failure on the stashed HEAD tree -- roughly one run in three fails, on a suite of 49. This CONFIRMS intermittency and removes any remaining reading that the fix wave caused it, but it also means a single green run is NOT evidence the flake is gone: whoever picks this up must run the suite several times, not once, before concluding anything. **INVESTIGATED FURTHER, 2026-08-10, alongside D-184's fix -- the hypothesis this row named was tested directly, not assumed.** Six `npx playwright test` full-suite runs on the UNFIXED tree (D-184's error still present, confirmed via `npm run lint` exit 1 throughout): 4 clean (49/49), 2 failed -- both on a DIFFERENT test, `e2e/two-orgs.spec.ts:76`, opened separately as D-188 since its mechanism (Resend's sandbox rejecting an email to a non-owner test address) shares nothing with this row's hypothesis. This row's own symptom -- `answerCurrentModule`'s `toBeChecked()` at `assessment-flow.spec.ts:218` -- did not reproduce even once in those 6 runs. D-184 was then fixed (see that row) and confirmed via `npm run lint` exit 0; `.next` was cleared (the known reused-server hazard this row already documents above) and 3 more full-suite runs were made on the FIXED tree: 2 clean (49/49), 1 failed -- and that failure WAS this row's exact symptom, same helper, same assertion, same locator-resolves-but-stays-unchecked signature, this time on `Q-PP-06` (a THIRD distinct question block, after Q-PP-01 and Q-PP-03 -- the pattern of which block fails keeps moving, further confirmation this is a genuine race and not a broken selector). **VERDICT: the hypothesis is not supported, and the direction of the evidence runs against it.** If D-184's cascading render were this row's cause, fixing it should have reduced or removed the failure; instead it occurred zero times in 6 runs before the fix and once in 3 runs after. Nine total runs is too small a sample to fit a precise rate to an event this infrequent, and this is NOT a claim that the fix made things worse -- but it is direct, sufficient evidence that eliminating the cascading render does not eliminate this row's symptom, so the two do not share the root this row speculated about. D-184 is closed on its own merits; this row's actual cause is still unknown and needs its own investigation, not a shared one  **UPDATED 2026-08-10 (Task 7): the investigation this trigger asked for has been done and the cause is known, so the pick-up is now a FIX, not a hunt.** Pick up when someone guards `AssessmentPageClient.tsx:205` against a superseded assessment response; the regression test is the deterministic probe preserved at `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-10-phase1d-i-e2e-trustworthy/task-7-artifacts/d185-probe.spec.ts`, which fails today and must pass after. Do NOT lift the quarantine on the strength of a green run: a longer timeout provably cannot fix this, and ten clean runs were measured on a tree where the bug was fully present. Separately, before the `test-e2e` job is ever treated as covering the assessment happy path, measure the assessment GET count against the PRODUCTION build (blocked locally, see the Why cell) -- if it is 1, this specific trigger cannot fire in CI and the quarantine may be liftable ahead of the product fix. | No longer coupled to D-184 (ruled out above) -- before relying on `npx playwright test` as an unattended/CI gate. Candidate next step: instrument the answering path directly (render-count or timestamp logging around `QuestionBlock`/the click handler) rather than reasoning about it from outside, since the one black-box hypothesis available has now been tried and falsified; alternatively, narrow the reproduction by running `assessment-flow.spec.ts` alongside one or two other spec files at a time (not the full 9-file suite) to find the minimal concurrent load that still triggers it, since the row already established the spec alone never fails -- Phase 1d -- the merge decision has now been made and did not resolve it  **MERGE-DECISION ADJUDICATION, 2026-08-10 (controller, at the C6 phase exit -- this row's own trigger).** NOT a merge blocker, and the reason is evidence rather than convenience. The earlier claim that this flake was pre-existing rested on stashing one agent's work at a downstream HEAD, which is the same flawed test that wrongly called the react-hooks lint error pre-existing (see D-184) -- so I ran the real check instead: `e2e/assessment-flow.spec.ts` at the merge base 8d1539a, where it FAILED on the first sampled run (`page.waitForURL: Test timeout of 90000ms exceeded`). The symptom differs from the one this row describes, so this is not proof the two are the same defect; what it does establish is that this spec file is unstable at the merge base, so the branch is not the origin of its instability. Merging does not make anything worse. What is NOT claimed: that the root cause is understood, or that a dropped radio click is only a harness race -- it is on the answer-recording path, which is the product's core interaction, and it stays open for that reason. **PHASE 1d-i QUARANTINE, 2026-08-10 (Task 2).** This test has been marked with `test.fixme()` at `e2e/assessment-flow.spec.ts:48` as PREPARATION for a future task that adds a `test-e2e` job to the required CI check. Playwright/e2e tests are currently NOT part of the required pipeline (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:212` states end-to-end tests are 'deliberately NOT' included, and per D-193 the current required check is `lint, typecheck, test, build` only); the `test-e2e` job does not yet exist. Quarantining this test now ensures it can be green on its first run without any flaky intermittency blocking the gate when that future task adds it. The quarantine mechanism is Playwright's native `test.fixme` annotation, which lists the test as expected-to-fail in every run output (thus keeping the quarantine visible, not silent). (Note: the commit message of 1bdf613 uses 'CI gate' language that this corrected text supersedes.) **COST STATEMENT:** This spec file contains exactly one test, which covers the entire assessment happy path from project creation through answering questions across multiple modules, completing the assessment, viewing the report, and downloading the PDF. Quarantining this test removes the ENTIRE assessment happy path from CI coverage, including the PDF download interaction which is the only e2e verification that the real PDF generation succeeds -- this is what the future `test-e2e` gate will NOT cover while this flake remains unfixed. The pick-up trigger is Task 7's investigation into the root cause. | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-186 | The four O-14 drift guards duplicate both the branch and the refusal response, and the test that pins them greps source text -- so consolidating them would fail a test even though nothing broke | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Raised independently by the altitude and reuse angles of the C6 fix wave's `simplify` pass, and recorded rather than fixed because the fix reaches into three already-reviewed Plan 1c sites. Two halves of one root. (a) All four guards return the byte-identical `{ kind: 'drifted' }` off the byte-identical condition, and their route-level responses are a hand-rolled `logSecurityEvent` plus a 409 repeated per site -- inconsistently, since only 2 of the 4 log at all (the evidence upload route and the new creation route do; the PATCH and complete routes do not). Nothing shared implements it; `lib/http/toResponse.ts` is the codebase's precedent for centralising error-to-response mapping but covers THROWN errors, not discriminated-union outcomes. The 6th copy is already foreseeable -- D-183's deferred draft export would copy it again. (b) `__tests__/integration/trigger-enumeration.test.ts` pins these four by grepping SOURCE TEXT for the guard line, and it is the only one of that file's enumerations that pins duplicated INLINE logic rather than callers of a NAMED function -- the idiom the same file uses successfully three times over (`bumpSessionEpoch`, `withDerivedEvidenceLevel`, `questionExistsInBank`). So extracting a shared `driftRefusal(resolution)` would collapse the grep from four hits to one and turn the test red while nothing had regressed: **the completeness check actively discourages the consolidation it should invite.** Already demonstrated fragile in this same wave -- two doc comments quoting the guard line inflated the expectation to six hits (documented in that test's own NOTE). Deliberately not fixed here: it means editing `respondToAssessment`, `completeAssessment` and the evidence upload route, none of which this wave's findings name, at a phase exit -- the scope-creep condition AGENTS.md §2 says to stop at | The next change that adds a FIFTH drift guard, or D-183 being picked up -- whichever comes first, because either one copies the pattern again. Do both halves in that change: extract the shared refusal, and convert the enumeration entry from a source-text grep to a caller enumeration of the extracted function, so the check stops penalising consolidation. Decide at the same time whether all four should log, since today two do and two do not for no stated reason | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-187 | The evidence upload UI's accepted-format list and the server's magic-byte table are independent hand-written lists with no mechanical link between them | RISK | 2026-08-09 | Raised by the altitude angle of the C6 fix wave's `simplify` pass, after F6 widened the UI list to include the OOXML family. F6 added a test pinning the `accept` attribute against the visible help text, so those two now agree by construction -- but a THIRD list exists and neither references it: `SIGNATURES` in `lib/evidence/inspect.ts`, the four magic-byte entries that are the actual server-side enforcement. All three agree today only because a human reasoned the mapping out by hand. The reuse angle argued these are legitimately separate sources of truth, and that is partly right -- the UI list is a picker hint carrying extensions and MIME types, while `SIGNATURES` carries raw byte prefixes that do not name formats, so one cannot simply be generated from the other (`PK\x03\x04` is `.docx`, `.xlsx`, `.pptx`, `.zip`, `.jar` and `.apk` alike, which is D-139's whole subject). The residual risk is still real and is what this row records: if `SIGNATURES` ever gains or loses an entry, nothing makes the UI follow, and the picker would silently offer or hide a format the server does not or does accept -- invisible to F6's test, which only checks the component agrees with itself. This is the same shape as the defect F6 fixed, one layer out | The next change to `SIGNATURES` in `lib/evidence/inspect.ts` -- adding or removing an accepted format. At that point add the missing link rather than editing two lists by hand: the tractable form is a single exported table mapping each signature to its user-facing format names and extensions, with `inspect()` and the UI both reading it, so the byte prefix and the picker hint cannot diverge. D-150 (plain text and CSV) is the most likely trigger to fire first | Phase 1d | Open |
| D-188 | `e2e/two-orgs.spec.ts`'s invitation-acceptance test fails intermittently -- Resend's sandbox mode refuses to send the invite email to the fixture's non-owner test address, so the accept link the test waits for never renders | BUG | 2026-08-10 | Found incidentally while gathering a baseline for D-185's flake investigation: 6 consecutive `npx playwright test` full-suite runs on the tree before D-184's fix, run purely to characterise D-185's radio-check symptom before touching any code. 2 of those 6 runs failed, both on the SAME test -- `e2e/two-orgs.spec.ts:76` ("an invitation moves a third person into an org... a brand-new person accepts a live invitation into org A as assessor") -- and both on the SAME assertion, `two-orgs.spec.ts:93`'s `expect(acceptLink).toBeVisible()`, timing out because `locator('a[href*="/invitations/"]').first()` never resolves. The dev-server log for both failures names the cause directly rather than leaving it inferred: an `ORG_MEMBER_ACTION`/`invite_email_failed` entry whose `error` field reads "Email send failed (validation_error): You can only send testing emails to your own email address (alvin.n256@gmail.com). Resend's sandbox mode will not deliver to any address but the developer's own verified one; the fixture invites a synthetic `two-orgs-third-<timestamp>@fixture.test` address, which that mode always rejects. Refusing to send email."`); with `env: { RESEND_API_KEY: '' }` temporarily removed, the SAME command reproduces this row's exact original symptom live -- the server log shows a REAL Resend rejection (`"Email send failed (validation_error): You can only send testing emails to your own email address..."`, the same shape as this row's own citation above) and the new `emailError` assertion fails with `Expected pattern: /RESEND_API_KEY/, Received string: "Email send failed (validation_error)..."` -- proving the assertion has real discriminating power, not just that it happens to pass. Restored, re-ran, passed again. A NEW integration test, `__tests__/integration/invite-email-failure.test.ts` (O-7), covers the route's tolerance of the email-leg throw directly (zero coverage before this task) -- its own non-vacuity was checked the same way: temporarily made the route's catch rethrow (`throw e;`), confirmed the test fails on an uncaught `Error: RESEND_API_KEY is not set...` (propagates because `lib/http/toResponse.ts:41` rethrows any error type it does not recognise), reverted, confirmed pass again. Full gates green: `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npx vitest run` (52 files / 1573 tests), `npx playwright test` (48 passed, 1 skipped -- the pre-existing D-185 `test.fixme()` quarantine, unrelated to this row). **Residual, consciously accepted, not a new row:** a developer's already-running local dev server started BEFORE this fix (`reuseExistingServer: true` locally) could still hold a real `RESEND_API_KEY` in its process env and call Resend for real on the next invite -- this is exactly the scenario the `emailError`-names-`RESEND_API_KEY` assertion exists to catch: it is DESIGNED to fail loud in that case (proven above), not to silently pass. CI is unaffected either way (`reuseExistingServer: !process.env.CI` is always false there, so every CI run starts fresh) | Before relying on `npx playwright test` as an unattended/CI gate, or before a second developer whose email is not the Resend sandbox's verified recipient runs this suite -- at that point every run of this test fails, not just some. | D-189 | O-23's dedup key omits `uploadedById`, so a second user re-uploading identical bytes silently inherits the first user's provenance | BUG | 2026-08-10 | Found by the C6 whole-branch security review, below its own reporting bar because there is no escalation — same org, same grants — but it is a provenance defect in the feature whose entire point is provenance. `createEvidence` (`lib/data/evidence.ts:33-41`) dedups on `(assessmentId, sha256, questionId, remediationItemId)` and returns the existing row. So if user B uploads a byte-identical file to a claim user A already evidenced, B receives A's row: the PDF manifest attributes the artifact to A, and B's own `/api/users/me/export` does not list it at all. Both are then false statements about who did what, in a document an ISO 42001 auditor reads for exactly that. Byte-identical re-upload is not exotic here — the likely case is two team members attaching the same policy PDF to related questions. Distinct from D-154, which covers the concurrency race in the same function, not the attribution semantics | Before the first pilot where more than one person uploads evidence, since that is when the two-uploader case becomes reachable at all. The fix is a decision, not a patch: either add `uploadedById` to the dedup key and accept duplicate bytes, or keep single-storage and record additional attributions separately. The first is simpler; the second is what an auditor would actually want, since it answers who relied on this artifact rather than only who arrived first | Phase 2 | Open |

Task 2 handled all four: it found this repo's real `.env`, containing a live-looking `RESEND_API_KEY`, being copied into the shipped Docker image, and closed it with a `.dockerignore` plus a `postbuild` step. Zero mentions across 40 commits and the full ledger. **The counterpoint that makes this worth recording rather than shrugging off:** the five skills newly bound to moments at the Plan 1a exit — `code-review`, `simplify`, `systematic-debugging`, `adversarial-reviewer`, `silent-failure-hunter` — each fired 0 times before being bound and all five fired repeatedly this phase, with `silent-failure-hunter` alone appearing 15 times and finding a real HIGH.
